# goip ↔ `ip` parity gaps: implementation status

**Last updated:** 2026-10-23

**Summary:**
- The design and the task plan are in [goip-parity-gaps.md](goip-parity-gaps.md). This document tracks progress against them.
- C0, the capture harness, is done and on `main` at `1542ec5f`: #265 (the plan), then #266 and #267 (C0-T01, C0-T02) through #268. A row names its netlink protocol (C0-T01), and the modules and sysctls the genl and XFRM rows need are set up after every existing row (C0-T02).
- C1, the rtnetlink captures, is done (closed at C1-T04, DOC004). C1-T01: the seven DEL and SET types the corpus had no capture of are captured under `link-write/rtnl/` and checked by `TestRtnlWriteCorpus`. C1-T02: 44 more rows there capture the class-B verbs goip lacks (neigh, rule, nexthop with NEXTHOPBUCKET, addrlabel, LINKPROP, token and route), checked by `TestRtnlWriteCorpusVerbs` and three more tests. C1-T03: a reads namespace captures the reads the dump corpus had none of (`mroute` empty and with an smcrouted cache entry, `mrule`, `link xstats` and `afstats`, `netns list-id`, `token`, and the link-dump half of `tunnel` and `tuntap`) under `dumps/reads/`, checked by `TestRtnlReadCorpus` and `TestRtnlReadCorpusDetail`. C1-T04: `ip monitor prefix` while a router advertisement arrives, the same RA with no subscriber, and `ip monitor all` (with and without `-t`) across every event type it prints, under `link-write/rtnl/`, checked by `TestRtnlMonitorCorpus`; NEWPREFIX is captured for the first time. From C1-T02 on, each task is committed to the working branch when it verifies, without a PR of its own.
- C2, the generic netlink captures, is done (closed at C2-T08, DOC005). C2-T01: 14 `ip tcp_metrics` rows (dumps, GETs, DELs and the three flush forms) over four entries made by local TCP connections, under `link-write/genl/`, each pcap with its family lookup, checked by `TestGenlTcpMetricsCorpus`; tcp_metrics' GET and DEL are captured for the first time. C2-T02: 11 `ip sr` rows (HMAC key dumps, sets, a replace, a delete, two the kernel refuses, a 64-byte secret, and the tunnel source's get and set), checked by `TestGenlSeg6Corpus`; all four SEG6 commands are captured for the first time. C2-T03: 21 `ip ioam` rows (namespaces, schemas and the binding between them, with five the kernel refuses, and `ip ioam monitor` while a traced packet arrives), checked by `TestGenlIoam6Corpus`; all seven IOAM6 commands and the TRACE event are captured for the first time. C2-T04: 27 `ip mptcp` rows (endpoints and limits, with seven refused, and `ip mptcp monitor` over a path-manager listener and over one connection with an extra subflow), checked by `TestGenlMptcpCorpus`; all seven mptcp_pm commands `ip` sends, and seven of its ten event types, are captured for the first time. C2-T05: 21 `ip fou` rows (ports added in each family, with a peer and with a device, deleted, dumped, and ten refused, by the kernel or by `ip` itself), checked by `TestGenlFouCorpus`; fou's three commands are captured for the first time. C2-T06: 15 `ip ila` rows (mappings added with and without a dev and with each option, deleted whether they match or not, dumped, and three refused), checked by `TestGenlIlaCorpus`; ila's three commands are captured for the first time. C2-T07: 24 `ip l2tp` rows (UDP, IPv6 and IP-encap tunnels, ethernet sessions with and without options, deletes, dumps with and without ids, a duplicate session the kernel takes, and eight refused), checked by `TestGenlL2tpCorpus`; the six l2tp commands `ip` sends are captured for the first time. C2-T08: 32 `ip macsec` rows (SecY dumps, tx and rx SAs and receive channels added, set and deleted, an XPN SA, offload, and sixteen refused, by the kernel or by `ip` itself), checked by `TestGenlMacsecCorpus`; all eleven macsec commands are captured for the first time. Every genl family `ip` uses now has captured bytes; none has a parser yet (P2). The parity matrix compares 183 commands and gates 160 ([coverage-status.md](coverage-status.md#goip--ip-parity-at-a-glance)).
- C3, the XFRM captures, is done (closed at C3-T02, DOC006). C3-T01: 29 `ip xfrm state` rows (SAs added with hmac, cbc, AEAD and encryption alone, updated, got, deleted, listed with and without filters, deleted all, flushed, counted, a larval state allocated and updated, and nine refused, by the kernel or by `ip` itself) under `link-write/xfrm/`, checked by `TestXfrmStateCorpus`; the seven XFRM types `ip xfrm state` sends are captured for the first time. C3-T02: 29 `ip xfrm policy` rows (policies added, updated, got, deleted, listed with filters, deleted all and flushed, SPDINFO counted and its hash thresholds set, the defaults set and got, and the refusals, by the kernel or by `ip` itself) and 7 `ip xfrm monitor` rows under `xtcp2-nl-xfrmmon` (an SA and a policy, ACQUIRE, EXPIRE, POLEXPIRE, NEWAE, and MAPPING from a hand-built ESP-in-UDP packet), checked by `TestXfrmPolicyCorpus`; 21 of the 22 XFRM types are captured, and REPORT, which only MIPv6 route optimization sends, is excluded (decision log).
- C4, the corpus completeness gate, is done (closed at C4-T01, DOC007), and with it Tier 1, the captures. `TestNetlinkUniverse` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_universe_test.go`) holds the 115 message types `ip` 7.1.0 names (47 rtnetlink, 46 genl, 22 XFRM) as a literal list, each with its iproute2 source line, walks every committed DLT 253 pcap, and fails if a type is in no pcap and not excluded, if an exclusion has no reason or is stale, or if the lists are malformed. Six types are excluded: three bridge-only rtnetlink dumps, DELNEXTHOPBUCKET, devlink's CTRL_CMD_GETPOLICY and XFRM's REPORT. Every other type is captured.
- P0, the benchmarks for the existing parsers, is done (closed at P0-T04, DOC008), and every parser in the assessment has a parse benchmark. P0-T01: `BenchmarkParseNewLink` and `BenchmarkParseNewRoute`, and `BenchmarkParseNewAddr` extended, each over the smallest, a typical and the largest body of its type the kernel sent in the corpus (`benchRecords`, `pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_parse_bench_test.go`), checked by `TestBenchPick` and `TestParseBenchRecords`. P0-T02: `BenchmarkParseNeigh`, `BenchmarkParseRule` and `BenchmarkParseNewNexthop`, with every P0 benchmark reading one `parseBenchCases` table whose types `TestParseBenchCases` pins, and `0 allocs/op` asserted for `ParseRule` (`TestParseBenchAllocs`). P0-T03: `BenchmarkParseNewAddrLabel`, `BenchmarkParseNewNeighTbl`, `BenchmarkParseNewNetconf` and `BenchmarkParseNewStats`, with `ParseNewStats` asserted allocation-free on its two header-only picks. P0-T04: `BenchmarkParseNlMsgErr`, `BenchmarkParseRtnetlinkEvent` (over DELLINK), `BenchmarkParseGenericNetlink` and `BenchmarkParseGenericNetlinkFamily` (over nlctrl's NEWFAMILY replies) and `BenchmarkParseEthtool`; a table row now names a kind, a protocol and either a type or a genl family resolved by name in each pcap, and `ParseNlMsgErr` is asserted allocation-free on its two capped picks.
- P1, the new rtnetlink parsers, is done (closed at P1-T04, DOC009), and every rtnetlink body layout in the assessment has a parser and a parse benchmark. P1-T01: `ParseNsid` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_nsid.go`) decodes RTM_NEWNSID, DELNSID and GETNSID, the kernel's attributes and `ip`'s request ones, checked by `TestParseNsid` (14 rows) and by `TestParseNsidCorpus` over all 415 captured NSID messages, with `BenchmarkParseNsid` allocation-free. P1-T02: `ParseNexthopBucket` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_nhbucket.go`) decodes NEWNEXTHOPBUCKET and ip's GETNEXTHOPBUCKET, checked by `TestParseNexthopBucket` (15 rows) and by `TestParseNexthopBucketCorpus`, which finds each of the 1024 buckets of C1-T02's dump once, with `BenchmarkParseNexthopBucket` allocation-free. P1-T03: `ParseLinkProp` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_linkprop.go`) decodes the NEWLINKPROP and DELLINKPROP requests only `ip` sends, checked by `TestParseLinkProp` (13 rows) and `TestParseLinkPropCorpus`; its benchmark runs over `ip`'s requests, through a new request kind. P1-T04: `ParsePrefix` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_prefixmsg.go`) decodes the kernel's RTM_NEWPREFIX, with the advertisement's flag byte passed through raw, checked by `TestParsePrefix` (10 rows) and `TestParsePrefixCorpus` over the 3 captured notifications; `TestRtnlMonitorCorpus` now reads NEWPREFIX through it.
- P2, the genl parsers, is done (closed at P2-T08, DOC010), and every genl family in the universe has a parser, a table test and a parse benchmark. P2-T01: `ParseTCPMetrics` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_tcp_metrics.go`) decodes tcp_metrics' GET replies and `ip`'s GET and DEL requests, built on `ParseGenericNetlink`, checked by `TestParseTCPMetrics` (21 rows) and by `TestParseTCPMetricsCorpus` over all 58 captured messages; `TestGenlTcpMetricsCorpus` now reads its addresses through it. P2-T02: `ParseSeg6` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_seg6.go`) decodes `ip sr`'s four SEG6 commands and the kernel's DUMPHMAC and GET_TUNSRC answers, checked by `TestParseSeg6` (21 rows) and by `TestParseSeg6Corpus` over all 25 captured messages; the attribute decoder is now `genlDecoder`, shared by the genl parsers. P2-T03: `ParseIOAM6` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_ioam6.go`) decodes `ip ioam`'s seven commands and the kernel's namespace and schema dumps, and `ParseIOAM6Event` the TRACE event, whose command and attribute numbers are their own, checked by `TestParseIOAM6` (25 rows), `TestParseIOAM6Event` (9) and `TestParseIOAM6Corpus` over all 43 captured messages. P2-T04: `ParseMPTCPPM` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_mptcp_pm.go`) decodes `ip mptcp`'s seven commands and the kernel's endpoint and limits answers, and `ParseMPTCPEvent` its events, told from answers by `nlmsg_pid` 0 since their numbers overlap, checked by `TestParseMPTCPPM` (29 rows), `TestParseMPTCPEvent` (15) and `TestParseMPTCPPMCorpus` over all 74 captured messages. P2-T05: `ParseFOU` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_fou.go`) decodes `ip fou`'s ADD, DEL and GET and the kernel's GET answers, checked by `TestParseFOU` (22 rows) and `TestParseFOUCorpus` over all 37 captured messages. P2-T06: `ParseILA` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_ila.go`) decodes `ip ila`'s ADD, DEL and GET and the kernel's GET answers, locators in `ip`'s network-order form, checked by `TestParseILA` (20 rows) and `TestParseILACorpus` over all 34 captured messages. P2-T07: `ParseL2TP` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_l2tp.go`) decodes `ip l2tp`'s six commands and the kernel's TUNNEL_GET and SESSION_GET answers, with the nested counters, checked by `TestParseL2TP` (28 rows) and `TestParseL2TPCorpus` over all 46 captured messages. P2-T08: `ParseMacsec` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_macsec.go`) decodes `ip macsec`'s eleven commands and the kernel's GET_TXSC dump, the SecY, its SA and channel lists and their counters, checked by `TestParseMacsec` (35 rows) and `TestParseMacsecCorpus` over all 57 captured messages.
- P3, the XFRM parsers, is done (closed at P3-T03, DOC011), and every XFRM type in the universe but the excluded REPORT has a parser, a table test and a parse benchmark. P3-T01: `ParseXfrmState` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_xfrm_state.go`) decodes the SA messages, NEWSA, UPDSA, DELSA, GETSA (a dump included), FLUSHSA, NEWAE and GETAE, each struct and its `XFRMA_*` attributes, on the constants, structs and attribute decoder `pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_xfrm.go` holds for the later tasks, checked by `TestParseXfrmState` (43 rows) and by `TestParseXfrmStateCorpus` over all 69 captured SA messages; `TestXfrmStateCorpus`, `TestXfrmPolicyCorpus` and `TestNetlinkUniverse` use the exported constants. P3-T02: `ParseXfrmPolicy` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_xfrm_policy.go`) decodes the policy messages, NEWPOLICY, UPDPOLICY, DELPOLICY, GETPOLICY (a dump included), FLUSHPOLICY, POLEXPIRE, SETDEFAULT and GETDEFAULT, with the templates, nested policy and policy type `XfrmAttrs` now carries, checked by `TestParseXfrmPolicy` (40 rows) and by `TestParseXfrmPolicyCorpus` over all 59 captured policy messages. P3-T03: `ParseXfrmState` gains ALLOCSPI's `xfrm_userspi_info`; `ParseXfrmInfo` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_xfrm_info.go`) decodes GETSADINFO, NEWSADINFO, GETSPDINFO and NEWSPDINFO, a u32 of flags then the SAD or SPD attributes; and `ParseXfrmEvent` (`pkg/xtcpnl/xtcpnl_xfrm_event.go`) decodes the kernel's ACQUIRE, EXPIRE and MAPPING, checked by `TestParseXfrmInfo` (23 rows), `TestParseXfrmEvent` (16), 5 more `TestParseXfrmState` rows, and corpus tests over every captured message of those types.
- G0, the matrix quick wins, is done (closed at G0-T03, DOC012), and the parity matrix gates 182 of its 183 commands; the one left, `addr show dynamic`, stays out by design. G0-T01: the twelve queued class-A rows (`-0 addr show`, the two `route show table` rows, `-4 route show`, `-4` and `-6 neigh show`, and the six `-j` rows) are in `gated_commands`, after two fresh `goip-parity` runs printed each `GOIP_PARITY_PASS` with no findings, checked by `TestQueuedReadRowsGated`; the ungated surface is eleven rows. G0-T02: `stats show` without a dev names its records as `ip` does, the `RTM_GETSTATS` dump and then one `RTM_GETLINK` get per uncached ifindex (`resolveStatsNames`, `internal/goip/obj_stats.go`), in place of an up-front link dump, checked byte for byte against the captured requests by `TestStatsDumpRequestsMatchIpBytes`; the six stats dump rows then read `txns` 6/6 and passed on two runs, and are gated, checked by `TestStatsDumpRowsGated`. G0-T03: the four rows left that could gate are gated. The two held-out `-s` forms gate on G0-T01's bar. The two netconf-dev rows gate once goip sends `ll_name_to_index`'s `RTM_GETLINK` by name ahead of the link dump, checked by `TestNetconfDevRequestsMatchIpBytes`, and once the segmenter closes an ACKed point get on its ACK, which had been an orphan on both sides of `-4 netconf show dev` (`awaitsAck`, with the ACK's echoed seq and pid normalized). Two runs ended `GOIP_PARITY_OVERALL_PASS`, 183 of 183, the first to; `TestNetconfDevAndSweepRowsGated` pins the flip, and the permanent set is empty.
- G1, the read verbs on running objects, is done, closed at G1-T04 and re-measured (DOC013). G1-T01: `goip route get` sends `iproute_get`'s `RTM_GETROUTE` single get, its `fibmatch`, `notify`, `from … iif` and `-6` forms, and prints `print_route`'s reply with the `mark`, `uid` and `cache` tokens a get carries (`internal/goip/route_get.go`), checked byte for byte against the six C1-T02 captures by `TestBuildGetRouteRequest` and `TestRouteGetMatchesIp`. Its six matrix rows passed two live runs, 189 of 189 on both, and are gated, checked by `TestRouteGetRowsGated`. G1-T02: `goip neigh get` resolves the device by name and sends `ipneigh_get`'s `RTM_GETNEIGH` single get (`internal/goip/neigh_get.go`), checked byte for byte against the two C1-T02 captures by `TestBuildGetNeighRequest` and `TestNeighGetMatchesIp`; its two rows passed two live runs, 191 of 191, and are gated, checked by `TestNeighGetRowsGated`. G1-T02 also sets `NETLINK_EXT_ACK` on goip's socket, as `ip` does, so a refusal carries the kernel's message. G1-T03: `goip nexthop get` sends the `RTM_GETNEXTHOP` single get `nexthop show id` already sent, and `goip nexthop bucket show` and `get` send `RTM_GETNEXTHOPBUCKET`'s dump and single get and print `print_nexthop_bucket`'s lines (`internal/goip/nexthop_get.go`), checked byte for byte against the four C1-T02 captures by `TestBuildDumpNexthopBucketRequest`, `TestBuildGetNexthopBucketRequest` and `TestNexthopGetMatchesIp`. Its four rows passed two live runs, 195 of 195, and are gated, checked by `TestNexthopGetRowsGated`. G1-T03 also has `FamilyHdrLen` model the bucket messages, without which the comparator compared a bucket request by length alone. G1-T04: `goip link xstats type bridge` and `type bridge_slave` send `RTM_GETSTATS`'s xstats dumps and print `bridge_print_xstats`' stanzas, naming each bridge device with `ll_index_to_name`'s get, and `goip link afstats` sends the AF_SPEC dump (`internal/goip/link_xstats.go`), checked byte for byte against the three C1-T03 reads captures by `TestLinkXstatsMatchesIp`; `ParseNewStats` now decodes the bridge stp body and walks AF_SPEC. Its three rows passed two live runs, 198 of 198, and are gated, checked by `TestLinkXstatsRowsGated`. The matrix gates 197 of 198.
- G2, route completion, is done, closed at G2-T03 and re-measured (DOC014). G2-T01: `goip route save` writes `route show`'s dump to stdout, the magic and each reply its filter keeps whole, and `goip route showdump` reads that file back and prints it with `print_route`, naming each device with one `RTM_GETLINK` get (`internal/goip/route_save.go`), checked against the C1-T02 `route_save` capture by `TestRouteSave` (`ip.save` byte for byte) and `TestRouteShowdump`. The comparator recognizes a dump file on stdout and compares it record by record, seq and pid left out (`stdout:dumprecords`, `internal/goipparity/stdout_dump.go`), and the runner carries `route_save`'s file in. Its three rows passed two live runs, 201 of 201, and are gated, checked by `TestRouteSaveRows`. G2-T02: `goip route restore` reads that file and writes each route back in `restore_handler`'s three passes, no-gateway routes first and gateway routes last, after `ll_init_map`'s link dump (`internal/goip/route_restore.go`), checked against the C1-T02 `route_restore` capture by `TestRouteRestoreMatchesIp`; its write row passed two live runs, 202 of 202, and is gated, checked by `TestRouteRestoreRow`. G2-T03: `goip route flush` dumps, copies each route it keeps into an `RTM_DELROUTE` and dumps again until nothing matches, as `iproute_flush` does, with `-s`'s round text (`internal/goip/route_flush.go`), checked against the three C1-T02 flush captures by `TestRouteFlushMatchesIp` and `TestRouteFlush`. A dump the kernel ends with an errno in its `NLMSG_DONE`, a missing table's ENOENT, is now an error to goip as to `ip` (`DumpRtnetlinkMsgsDoneErr`), with `route show`'s and `route save`'s exception for main. Its four rows passed two live runs, 206 of 206, and are gated, checked by `TestRouteFlushRows`. The matrix gates 205 of 206.
- G3, rtnetlink writes on running objects, is done, closed at G3-T06 and re-measured (DOC015). G3-T01: `goip neigh add`, `change`, `replace` and `delete` send `ll_init_map`'s link dump and write `ipneigh_modify`'s one `RTM_NEWNEIGH` or `RTM_DELNEIGH` to `GOIP_WRITE_OUT` (`internal/goip/neigh_write.go`), checked against the six C1-T01 and C1-T02 neigh write captures by `TestNeighModifyMatchesIp`, and `goip neigh flush` dumps, copies each entry flush's state filter keeps into an `RTM_DELNEIGH` and dumps again, as `do_show_or_flush` does (`internal/goip/neigh_flush.go`), checked against `neigh_flush` by `TestNeighFlushMatchesIp`. The write gate admits `RTM_NEWNEIGH` and `RTM_DELNEIGH`. Its nine rows passed two live runs, 215 of 215, and are gated, checked by `TestNeighWriteRows`. G3-T02: `goip rule add` and `delete` write `iprule_modify`'s one `RTM_NEWRULE` or `RTM_DELRULE` to `GOIP_WRITE_OUT`, with no link dump (`internal/goip/rule_write.go`), checked against the seventeen C1-T01 and C1-T02 rule write captures by `TestRuleModifyMatchesIp`; `goip rule flush` copies each dumped rule carrying `FRA_PRIORITY` into an acked `RTM_DELRULE`, `goip rule save` writes the magic and every reply, and `goip rule restore` sends `ll_init_map`'s link dump and each saved rule back (`internal/goip/rule_save.go`), checked by `TestRuleFlushMatchesIp` and `TestRuleRestoreMatchesIp`. The write gate admits `RTM_NEWRULE` and `RTM_DELRULE`. Its twenty rows passed two live runs, 235 of 235, and are gated, checked by `TestRuleWriteRows`. G3-T03: `goip nexthop add`, `replace` and `delete` write `ipnh_modify`'s one `RTM_NEWNEXTHOP` or `RTM_DELNEXTHOP` to `GOIP_WRITE_OUT`, after `dev`'s by-name `RTM_GETLINK`, single, mpath group and resilient group with its nested `NHA_RES_GROUP` (`internal/goip/nexthop_write.go`), checked against the six C1-T01 and C1-T02 nexthop write captures by `TestNexthopModifyMatchesIp`; `goip nexthop flush` dumps the groups, then everything, and deletes each id once in an acked `RTM_DELNEXTHOP`, checked against `nexthop_flush` by `TestNexthopFlushMatchesIp`. The write gate admits `RTM_NEWNEXTHOP` and `RTM_DELNEXTHOP`. Its eight rows passed two live runs, 243 of 243, and are gated, checked by `TestNexthopWriteRows`. G3-T04: `goip addrlabel add` and `delete` write `ipaddrlabel_modify`'s one `RTM_NEWADDRLABEL` or `RTM_DELADDRLABEL` to `GOIP_WRITE_OUT`, and `goip addrlabel flush` sends the listing's dump and copies each reply carrying `IFAL_ADDRESS` into an acked `RTM_DELADDRLABEL` (`internal/goip/addrlabel_write.go`), checked against the three C1 addrlabel captures by `TestAddrLabelModifyMatchesIp` and `TestAddrLabelFlushMatchesIp`. The write gate admits `RTM_NEWADDRLABEL` and `RTM_DELADDRLABEL`. Its three rows passed two live runs, 246 of 246, and are gated, checked by `TestAddrLabelWriteRows`. G3-T05: `goip ntable change` sends `ll_init_map`'s link dump and writes `ipntable_modify`'s one `RTM_SETNEIGHTBL` to `GOIP_WRITE_OUT`, the table's attributes then the device's in an unflagged `NDTA_PARMS` (`internal/goip/ntable_write.go`), and `goip stats set` sends `dev`'s by-name `RTM_GETLINK` and writes `ipstats_set_do`'s one `RTM_SETSTATS` (`internal/goip/stats_set.go`), checked against the four C1-T02 captures by `TestNTableChangeMatchesIp` and `TestStatsSetMatchesIp`. The write gate admits `RTM_SETNEIGHTBL` and `RTM_SETSTATS`, and `FamilyHdrLen` models `RTM_SETSTATS`. Its four rows passed two live runs, 250 of 250, and are gated, checked by `TestNTableStatsWriteRows`. G3-T06: `goip link property add` and `del` build `iplink_prop_mod`'s `IFLA_PROP_LIST` nest of altnames, send `dev`'s by-name `RTM_GETLINK` and write one `RTM_NEWLINKPROP` or `RTM_DELLINKPROP` to `GOIP_WRITE_OUT` (`internal/goip/link_property.go`), checked against the three C1-T02 `link_altname_*` captures by `TestLinkPropertyMatchesIp`. The write gate admits `RTM_NEWLINKPROP` and `RTM_DELLINKPROP`, and `FamilyHdrLen` models both. Its three rows passed two live runs, 253 of 253, and are gated, checked by `TestLinkPropertyWriteRows`. The matrix gates 252 of 253, and the write gate admits 18 types, 6 before G3.
- G4, new rtnetlink objects, is done, closed at G4-T06 and re-measured (DOC016). G4-T01: `goip mroute show` sends `ll_init_map`'s link dump, then `mroute_list`'s `RTM_GETROUTE` dump of `RTNL_FAMILY_IPMR` with `RTA_TABLE` 253, or of `RTNL_FAMILY_IP6MR` under `-6`, and prints `print_mroute`'s lines and `-j` array, with the `table`, `iif`, `from` and `to` filters, `-s` and `-d` (`internal/goip/obj_mroute.go`); `ParseNewRoute` decodes `RTA_MFC_STATS` and `RTA_EXPIRES`. Checked against the three C1-T03 captures byte for byte by `TestMrouteMatchesIp`, and against iproute2-7.1.0 with a live smcrouted. A read row's Setup can now hold smcrouted in its namespace (`smcroute`); its six rows passed two live runs, 259 of 259 on both, and are gated, checked by `TestMrouteRows`. G4-T02: `goip mrule show` is `rule show` with `do_multirule`'s family, `RTNL_FAMILY_IPMR` or under `-6` `RTNL_FAMILY_IP6MR`, `-0` refused with exit 255 (`internal/goip/obj_mrule.go`), checked against the C1-T03 capture byte for byte by `TestMruleMatchesIp` and against iproute2-7.1.0; its four rows passed two live runs, 263 of 263 on both, and are gated, checked by `TestMruleRows`. G4-T03: `goip token` lists and sets the IPv6 interface token, `ll_init_map`'s link dump then the `AF_INET6` link dump read for `IFLA_INET6_TOKEN` (`ParseLinkToken`), or one `RTM_SETLINK` (`internal/goip/obj_token.go`), checked against the three C1 captures byte for byte and against iproute2-7.1.0; its five rows passed two live runs, 268 of 268 on both, and are gated, checked by `TestTokenRows`. G4-T04: `goip netns list-id` sends `netns_map_init`'s `RTM_GETNSID` by `NETNSA_FD` for each file under `/run/netns`, then the dump or the get by nsid, and prints `print_nsid`'s lines and `-j` array, naming each from the cache or a second walk; `goip netns set` sends one `RTM_NEWNSID` (`internal/goip/obj_netns.go`). Checked against the C1 captures byte for byte bar the descriptor and against iproute2-7.1.0; `NETNSA_FD` is an accepted divergence, the allowlist's first. Its five rows passed two live runs, 273 of 273 on both, and are gated, checked by `TestNetnsRows`. G4-T05: `RTM_NEWRULE` and `RTM_DELRULE` are event types, decoded by `ParseRtnetlinkEvent` into a `RuleEvent` with `ParseRule`, checked over `monitor_all`'s rule notification (`TestRuleEventsRealFixture`); the daemon's listener joins no rule group and is unchanged. G4-T06: `goip monitor` subscribes to `do_ipmonitor`'s groups on a socket of its own, fills the link cache by a dump on it, and prints each notification as `accept_msg` does, with `print_headers`' `-t`, `-ts`, nsid and label prefixes, the `dev` and family filters, and `file FILE`'s `rtnl_from_file` walk (`internal/goip/obj_monitor.go`, `source_monitor.go`); `ParseNewStats` decodes `hw_s_info` and `ParseNewLink` `IFLA_EVENT`. Checked against the seven monitor captures through `Run` (`TestMonitorMatchesIp`) and against iproute2-7.1.0 live, 41 of 41 outputs the same; the parity driver runs no monitor, so it has no matrix row. The matrix gates 272 of 273, goip runs 16 of 31 objects, and the write gate admits 20 types.
- G5's tasks: G5-T01: `pkg/nlparity` reads `NETLINK_GENERIC` captures, labels each genl message with the family name the capture's `CTRL_CMD_NEWFAMILY` replies give its id, and compares it by that name and its command (`GENL(fou).cmd(1)`), with `FamilyHdrLen` four bytes of `genlmsghdr`, no rtnetlink names or normalization for a genl id that shares an rtnetlink number, and the controller's family and group ids normalized (`pkg/nlparity/nlparity_genl.go`). Checked over the 165 C2 rows' captures, each clean against itself and against a copy whose family ids were moved, and over `fou add` against edited copies. G5-T02: goip opens a `NETLINK_GENERIC` socket on first use with `rtnl_open_byproto`'s options, resolves a family as `genl_resolve_family` does, one version-0 `CTRL_CMD_GETFAMILY` cached for the run, and dumps and talks on it (`GenlSource`); a replay answers a genl request from the recorded exchange with the same bytes (`internal/goip/source_genl.go`). Checked against every GETFAMILY in the C2 `ip.pcap`s byte for byte, and live against `genl ctrl` and an strace of `ip`.
- G5, the genl matrix harness, is done, closed at G5-T03 and re-measured (DOC017). G5-T03: a matrix row names its protocol, the comparator reads a genl row's captures as genl, and the parity driver captures it with `--proto generic`. Before the first genl row the driver loads the C0-T02 modules and sets `net.mptcp.enabled`; the table puts every genl row after every route row. Eight ungated stand-in rows, one `show` per family, run `ip` in goip's place, and G6 replaces them. `token_set`'s veths get `addrgenmode none`, which ends a DAD and router solicitation race in its link dump. With the fix, two consecutive live runs passed 281 of 281, identical row for row: the eight stand-ins clean, late setup's 17 modules loaded with no problem reported, and every route row passing as before. The matrix has 281 rows, 272 gated, and nine compared but not gated.
- G6's tasks: G6-T01: the genl write path, and `goip tcp_metrics`. A genl write is admitted by its family's name and command, and a `GOIP_WRITE_OUT` file of genl requests is compared by the family names goip's capture resolved. `goip tcp_metrics` shows, gets, deletes and flushes as `ip` does (`internal/goip/obj_tcp_metrics.go`), checked byte for byte against the 14 C2 captures by `TestTCPMetricsMatchesIp`. The comparator zeroes an entry's measured values and pairs entries by address and source. The stand-in is replaced by the 14 `tcpm_*` rows, eight reads and six dry-run writes, each on its corpus precondition through a new `tcp` Setup step. Two consecutive live runs passed 294 of 294, 286 gated. G6-T02: `goip sr`. Its `hmac set` and `tunsrc set` are admitted genl writes, and `hmac set` reads its secret as glibc's `getpass` does, from the terminal with echo off or, with none, from stdin (`internal/goip/obj_sr.go`), checked byte for byte against the 11 C2 captures by `TestSrMatchesIp`. A matrix row can carry a stdin line, the table's tenth field, which the driver pipes to each side under `setsid`. The stand-in is replaced by the 11 `sr_*` rows, four reads and seven dry-run writes, the keys they start from made by a new `hmac` Setup step. Two consecutive live runs passed 304 of 304, 297 gated. G6-T03: `goip ioam`. Its namespace and schema adds, deletes and binding are admitted genl writes, its shows print `ip`'s lines and JSON, and its monitor joins `ioam6_events` on the genl socket and prints each TRACE event (`internal/goip/obj_ioam.go`), checked byte for byte against the 21 C2 captures by `TestIoamMatchesIp`. The stand-in is replaced by the 18 `ioam_*` rows that are not the monitor's, two reads and 16 dry-run writes, and two `-j` shows; the driver runs no monitor, so its three rows are replayed and not in the matrix. Two consecutive live runs passed 323 of 323, 317 gated.
- The work runs in three tiers, in this order: complete the nlmon capture corpus (C), then the parsers (P), then goip and the matrix (G).

**Next action:** [G6-T04](goip-parity-gaps.md#g6--genl-objects), `goip mptcp`.

## Update rules

- States are `not started`, `in progress`, `blocked`, `done` or `deferred`. There are no percentages.
- A task is `done` only when its "Done when" has passed and the evidence is in the [ledger](#verification-ledger).
- Evidence IDs (`V###` for code and VM runs, `DOC###` for docs) are immutable and append-only.
- Task IDs (`Cn-Tmm`, `Pn-Tmm`, `Gn-Tmm`) are never renumbered.
- Every task's commit flips its own checklist row and appends its `V###` rows.
- A phase's last task also closes the phase, following [Closing a phase](goip-parity-gaps.md#closing-a-phase):
  - it re-measures the assessment;
  - it updates the phase states here;
  - it breaks the next phase into task rows.
- A statement elsewhere in `docs/netlink/` that a task makes false is amended in that task's commit.
