## Decision log

**2026-10-19:**
- **Class F is out of scope.** The ioctl, sysfs, `/proc`, syscall, cgroup and `bpf()` surfaces have no netlink message, so nlmon can't capture them and the pcap-based matrix can't compare them. If they are ever wanted, an strace capture (the RDMA route, `pkg/nlstrace`) is the starting point, as a separate plan.
- **Captures first.** The user directed that the corpus be completed before the parsers, and the parsers before goip, because committed testdata and parsers make the rest easier. The tiers are ordered C, then P, then G accordingly.
- **The universe is measured at the pin.** It is v7.1.0, not the 7.2 checkout. maddress is therefore class F until the pin bump.
- **The status lives in its own document.** It is split out of the plan the way [addr-write-path-status.md](addr-write-path-status.md) is. A phase is closed in its last task PR, not in a separate docs PR.
- **Capture modules are loaded late, by the drivers, not at boot.** A module that creates per-namespace fallback devices puts them in every namespace, including ones that already exist. Loading the tunnel modules at boot was measured to move goip0 from ifindex 3 to 10, which would rewrite every committed fixture (`nix/microvms/mkVm.nix:4034-4049`). C0-T02 therefore modprobes after every existing row is captured, as `capture-netlink-dumps.exp:775` already does for the tunnel modules.
- **C0-T01 fixes the nexthop floors it found.** The capture gate couldn't pass without the fix. Eight rows had a floor of 4 in namespaces whose nexthop table is empty, so every full `netlink-dump-capture` run ended `NLCAP_DRIVER_FAIL`. An empty table's dump is its request and NLMSG_DONE, so the floor there is 2 (`nh_floor`, `capture-netlink-dumps.exp`).
- **C0-T01 fixes the tier2 lint findings `main` carried.** lint-baseline is one of its gates, and `main` at `b7eec04d` failed it with 17 findings in `internal/goip` (V006). They were fixed in the code rather than baselined: `showObjects` is derived from the objects table (with an `alias` marker), the goiphttp registry defaults `object` to `path`, and the exhaustive, prealloc and unconvert sites were corrected (V007).
- **C0-T02 loads the genl and XFRM modules in one late step, shared by both drivers.** `nltopo::late_setup` runs after every existing row, into `late/`, so nothing already captured sees them. The list is `nltopo::late_modules`; the tunnel modules stay where they were, loaded before the dump driver's tunnel set. The crypto list is cbc(aes)+hmac(sha256) and rfc4106(gcm(aes)) ESP; C3 adds to it if its rows need more. Its sidecar says `loaded` or `builtin`, not raw `lsmod` output, because a built-in module is a pass that `lsmod` doesn't list.
- **C0 closed with C2 expanded.** C2's tasks were already named in the plan's table, so its range row became C2-T01 to C2-T08. C1 and C3 already had one row per task.
- **C0 reached `main` through a follow-up PR.** #266 and #267 were opened stacked, on `docs/goip-parity-gaps` and `goip-gaps/c0-t01`. They were merged after #265 without being retargeted, so they landed in those branches, and #268 brought `goip-gaps/c0-t01` into `main`. Task PRs are now opened against `main`.
- **C1-T01 has a SETNEIGHTBL row the plan did not name.** The kernel's `neightbl_set` refuses the table-wide thresholds (`thresh1`-`3`, `gc_int`) outside the initial namespace with ENOENT, and every capture runs in a namespace of its own. The accepted row therefore changes a per-device parameter (`dev d0 queue 64`), and `thresh1` is kept as a refused row, which records the refusal.
- **The C1-T01 netns rows add, use and delete their peer in one command.** `xtcp2-nlcap write` runs the command under `ip netns exec`, whose mount namespace is a slave of the root's. A peer that `ip netns add` makes there is a bind mount only that mount namespace sees, so a pre step cannot make it for the write, and a separate readback cannot see it. `netns_set` runs its `ip netns list-id` readback inside the command, and both rows delete the peer, which also removes `/run/netns/<peer>`.
- **The C1-T01 event rows' floors are two thirds of the first passing run** (14 of 21, 20 of 30), as `addr_monitor`'s are. The other floors are structural. The first run measured that `ip ntable` dumps the links before it writes (`ll_init_map`), even when it names no device, so every ntable row's floor is 4.
- **From C1-T02, tasks are committed without a PR each.** The per-task PR cycle was slow, so each task is committed to the working branch once its gates pass, with its checklist row and ledger rows in the same commit. Pushing and opening a PR happen when asked.
- **C1-T02 splits the empty route flush in two.** A flush of a table that was never made is a dump the kernel refuses with ENOENT ("FIB table does not exist", in NLMSG_DONE), rc 2. The boundary row the plan asked for, a flush with nothing to delete, makes table 100 and empties it first (a table outlives its last route), and the never-made table is kept as the refused row `route_flush_no_table`.
- **C1-T02 records that `ip rule restore` duplicates rules.** Restore sends NEWRULE with `NLM_F_CREATE` and no `NLM_F_EXCL`, and the kernel only refuses a duplicate rule under `NLM_F_EXCL`. Restoring into a namespace that already has the default rules therefore gives main and default twice, and the saved pref 0 rule, whose message has no `FRA_PRIORITY`, comes back at an auto-assigned 32765. The row keeps that behavior and `TestRtnlWriteCorpusShapes` pins it. goip's restore (G3) should match `ip`, not correct it.
- **C1-T02 leaves DELNEXTHOPBUCKET (117) at 0.** The kernel registers no handler for it and never sends it (`net/ipv4/nexthop.c` emits only NEWNEXTHOPBUCKET), so there are no bytes to capture. C4's universe test lists it as an exclusion.
- **C1-T03 keeps an smcrouted daemon running while it reads.** The kernel drops an (S,G) cache entry when the socket that added it closes, so a one-shot `smcroutectl` or `ip` cannot leave one to read. `xtcp2-nlcap mroute-start` runs `smcrouted` in the namespace until `mroute-stop`, and its VIF devices are veths, since a dummy has no `IFF_MULTICAST`.
- **C1-T03 captures only the netlink half of `tunnel show` and `tuntap show`.** Both list links by dump, then read each tunnel's parameters through `SIOCGETTUNNEL` and each tap's flags from sysfs, neither of which nlmon sees. The pcaps are committed for the dump; the ioctl and sysfs halves stay in [class F](goip-parity-gaps.md#f-not-netlink-outside-pcap-parity), and the topology sidecar records the note.
- **C1-T04's event rows are in the link-write corpus, not the nlmon-capture flavor.** The plan named `microvm-x86_64-nlmon-capture`, whose oneshot captures one whole boot. The C1-T01 event-row form (`xtcp2-nl-ipmon` with a canary multicast join, one row per pcap with its topology, stdout and rc) gives each event its own fixture, and the driver already gates floors and statuses.
- **The NEWPREFIX trigger is a router advertisement sent inside the namespace.** `xtcp2-nl-ra` sends from `r0p` to its veth peer `r0`. The kernel drops an RA whose source is one of its own addresses unless `accept_ra_from_local` is 1, and both ends are in the namespace; `r0` also needs `accept_ra` 2 in case forwarding is on. Multicast loopback is off, so `r0p` does not receive its own RA and notify a second NEWPREFIX.
- **`ip` prints no `onlink` or `autoconf` for a received RA, and goip should match.** The kernel copies the option's flags byte into `prefix_flags` as it is on the wire (L 0x80, A 0x40), but `ip/ipprefix.c` tests `IF_PREFIX_ONLINK` (0x01) and `IF_PREFIX_AUTOCONF` (0x02). The output also has no space before `dev`. `TestRtnlMonitorCorpus` pins both.
- **`ip monitor all` takes no `maddress`.** Any object named on the command line replaces the default set, so `all maddress` is maddress alone. `all` already includes it, and it is the only group the canary needs.
- **The NEWNEXTHOP notification has `NLM_F_REQUEST` set.** `nexthop_notify` copies the request's `nlmsg_flags` into its event (`net/ipv4/nexthop.c:1091`), so a listener can't tell a nexthop event from a request by its flags alone. The test tells them apart by `nlmsg_pid`, which `ip` sends as 0 and the kernel sets to the requester's port. goip's event decoding (G1 on) must not filter on `NLM_F_REQUEST`.
- **C1 closed without expanding C2.** C2's tasks were already one row each (C0's close).
- **C2-T01 makes its tcp_metrics entries over local addresses, not a veth pair.** The kernel saves an entry when a connection with an RTT sample closes, whatever device carried it, so two addresses on a dummy, with the namespace's `lo` carrying the traffic, give the same four entries (one per end per family) with less to build. The genl rows run after late setup, as every row added since C0-T02 does.
- **`ip tcp_metrics` skips the word after an address, and goip need not.** The argument loop in `ip/tcp_metrics.c` (v7.1.0) advances in its body and again in its `for`, so `show address A src B` reads B as a second address and exits 255 (`either "address" is duplicate`), while `show src B address A` works. The `src` rows put `src` first. A goip `tcp_metrics` parity row should use that order.
- **`prefix_event`'s floor is 11, not 18.** C1-T04 set it to two thirds of a run whose canary had gone round twice (27 datagrams). With one canary cycle the row is 17, which V043 and V044 measured three times, so 18 failed every run. The committed fixture, from the two-cycle run, stays.
- **A walking flush sends its DELs without `NLM_F_ACK`.** `flush <prefix>` and `-6 flush` batch one DEL per match into a datagram with no ACK asked for, then dump again; `flush all` with no family is a single DEL that asks for one. The test pins both.
- **`ip sr hmac set` gets its secret from stdin under `setsid`.** It reads the secret with `getpass(3)`, which uses `/dev/tty` whenever the process has a controlling terminal, so a pipe alone is ignored under an interactive driver. `setsid -w` takes the terminal away; `getpass` then reads stdin and prompts on stderr, which is why every set row's `ip.err` holds the prompt.
- **`ip sr`'s refused writes exit 1, and goip should match.** `ip/ipseg6.c` returns -1 from a failed SETHMAC or SET_TUNSRC, so `ip` exits 1, where the rtnetlink and tcp_metrics commands exit 2 for a refused request. The two refused rows pin rc 1.
- **`ip` shows at most 63 bytes of an HMAC secret, and goip should match.** The kernel takes and returns up to 64 (`SEG6_HMAC_SECRET_LEN`), but `ip/ipseg6.c` copies the secret into a 64-byte string buffer, so it prints 63 and warns `HMAC secret length 64 > 63, truncated` on stderr. `sr_hmac_show_64` was added for this.
- **`ip ioam monitor` is captured by sending the kernel a traced packet.** `IOAM6_EVENT_TRACE` is sent by `ipv6_hop_ioam` (`net/ipv6/exthdrs.c`) when an IPv6 packet whose hop-by-hop header holds a 4n-aligned IOAM pre-allocated trace option arrives on a device with `ioam6_enabled`, for a namespace the kernel knows, while someone listens. The ioam6 lwtunnel only inserts that option, so its absence from the guest kernel (`IPV6_IOAM6_LWTUNNEL`) doesn't matter: `xtcp2-nl-ioam` writes the option itself with `IPV6_HOPOPTS` on a UDP socket to `::1`. That needs CAP_NET_RAW, which the check sandbox lacks, so the helper's send is exercised in the VM, and the sandbox pins its bytes and its refusal. A first draft of this task wrongly read the lwtunnel as the event's only source and left the monitor out; reading `exthdrs.c` corrected it before the commit. Two rows pin when no event comes: an unknown namespace, and `ioam6_enabled` off.
- **The kernel hides IOAM6 data equal to its "unavailable" value, and goip should print what the dump holds.** A namespace with no data is stored with all ones, and the dump leaves an all-ones value out, so `data 0xffffffff wide ffffffffffffffff` reads back as no data. The kernel also pads schema data with zeros to a multiple of 4, so 5 bytes read back as 8. The test pins both.
- **`ip mptcp`'s refused requests exit 2, and its refused arguments 255, and goip should match.** `ip/ipmptcp.c` returns -2 when the kernel refuses, as tcp_metrics does. It checks some arguments itself after the family lookup: a port without `signal` is `invarg`, exit 255, with nothing sent. The row pins both the status and the message.
- **MPTCP_PM_SUBFLOWS_MAX is 8 in 7.1 and 64 from 7.2, and the rows pin 7.1's.** `limits set subflows 9` is refused on the guest's 7.1.4 (V059); the kernel tree read for this task (7.3-rc7) has 64, from "mptcp: pm: in-kernel: increase all limits to 64". A pin bump past 7.1 moves the boundary rows.
- **The mptcp monitor rows pin the events from the path manager's own listener and from one connection; SF_PRIO, ANNOUNCED and REMOVED are not captured.** Those need a backup change on a live subflow, or a peer announcing or withdrawing an address, which means holding a connection open across commands, or two path managers, in one namespace. Both ends of the connection share the namespace's endpoints, so the server's path manager also tries a subflow back to the client's ephemeral port and logs an SF_CLOSED with ECONNREFUSED; the test pins it. The userspace path manager's commands (ANNOUNCE, REMOVE, SUBFLOW_CREATE, SUBFLOW_DESTROY) are not `ip` commands, so they are outside the universe.
- **`ip fou` sends an empty ADD when it refuses its own arguments, and goip should not copy that.** `fou_parse_opt` prints `fou: missing port` or `fou: must set ipproto or gue` and returns -1, but `do_add` ignores the return and sends the request anyway, with no attribute, since the complaint comes before the first one is added. The kernel refuses it with `EINVAL` (no type), so `ip` prints its own complaint and then `RTNETLINK answers: Invalid argument`, and exits 2 (`fou_add_no_port`, `fou_add_no_type`). goip should refuse such arguments without a request; the parity comparison of these two rows is on stderr's first line and the rc, not on the wire.
- **`ip fou`'s own refusals exit 255 or 1, and goip should match.** Port 0 is `invarg` (255). An unknown dev is `exit(EXIT_FAILURE)` (1), and `show` with an argument returns -1 from `do_show`, which `ip` negates to 1. Each is after the family lookup, with nothing more sent.
- **A fou GET that is not a dump, and `FOU_ATTR_REMCSUM_NOPARTIAL`, are not captured.** `ip/ipfou.c` sends GET only as a dump, and has no keyword for the flag, so neither is reachable from `ip`; the kernel's `fou_nl_get_doit` and the flag's parse path stay without bytes. The corner row in `TestGenlFouCorpus` pins that no row sends either.
- **`ip ila` sends an empty ADD when it refuses its own arguments, and the kernel takes it.** As with fou, `ila_parse_opt`'s -1 is ignored, so `ip ila add loc_match X` prints `ila: missing locator` and sends an ADD with no attribute. Unlike fou's, the kernel accepts it, as a mapping of locator 0 to locator 0 with its default csum-mode and ident-type, and `ip` exits 0 (`ila_add_no_loc`). By the code, a bad locator, csum-mode, ident-type or dev does the same; those are not captured. goip should refuse such arguments without a request rather than create the zero mapping; how the parity row compares a deliberate difference is for G to decide.
- **Every `ip ila del` exits 0, and goip should match.** `ila_xlat_nl_cmd_del_mapping` returns 0 whatever `ila_del_mapping` found, so a delete of a mapping that is not there, or one that misses on the dev, is ACKed (`ila_del_missing`, `ila_del_nodev_of_dev`).
- **`ip ila show` prints csum-mode and ident-type with nothing between them, and goip should match it.** `print_ila_mapping` prints each with a bare `%s`, so a line ends `no-actionuse-format`. The parity matrix compares text, so goip prints the same; the JSON output keeps them as separate keys.
- **An ila GET that is not a dump, and FLUSH, are not captured.** `ip/ipila.c` sends GET only as a dump and has no flush command, so `ila_xlat_nl_cmd_get_mapping` and `ila_xlat_nl_cmd_flush` stay without bytes. The corner row in `TestGenlIlaCorpus` pins that no row sends either.
- **A duplicate L2TPv3 session id in a UDP tunnel is taken, even in the same tunnel, and the dump then shows only one of the two.** `l2tp_session_register` puts a UDP tunnel's duplicate on a collision list, refusing it (`EEXIST`) only when the existing session is in an IP-encap tunnel. `l2tp_v3_session_get_next` assumes one session per id per tunnel and stops at the first hash match, the newer, so `ip l2tp show session` lists `l2tpeth1` and not `l2tpeth0` (`l2tp_session_add_dup`, `l2tp_session_add_dup_ip`). The fixtures pin what kernel 7.1.4 does; goip should match the dump it gets, not the sessions it made.
- **`ip l2tp show` with ids is a dump, and goip should match it.** `ip/ipl2tp.c` always sends GET with `NLM_F_DUMP` and puts the ids in the request; the kernel's dump handlers do not read them, and `ip` prints every tunnel or session that comes back. A GET that is not a dump (`l2tp_nl_cmd_tunnel_get`, `l2tp_nl_cmd_session_get`) is therefore never sent, and has no bytes.
- **TUNNEL_MODIFY, SESSION_MODIFY and NOOP are not captured.** `ip l2tp` has no command that sends them. The corner row in `TestGenlL2tpCorpus` pins that no row does.
- **l2tp's multicast notifications are not captured.** The kernel sends a copy of each create and delete to the `l2tp` multicast group (`l2tp_tunnel_notify`, `l2tp_session_notify`), but only to listeners, and no row opens one, as `ip` has no l2tp monitor. A row with a listener is G's to add, if goip grows a monitor.
- **`ip macsec show DEV` is a dump, and goip should match it.** `ip/ipmacsec.c` sends GET_TXSC only with `NLM_F_DUMP` and prints the SecY whose ifindex matches, so `show macsec1` gets every SecY back (`macsec_show_dev`). macsec's GET_TXSC has no handler for a GET that is not a dump.
- **The kernel returns a key id but never a key, and an SSCI but never a salt.** `dump_secy` writes `MACSEC_SA_ATTR_KEYID` and, on an XPN SecY, `MACSEC_SA_ATTR_SSCI`, and leaves the key and salt out, so `ip` prints the key id after `key`. goip prints what the dump holds.
- **A SecY's `oper` follows its encoding SA, and starts true.** `macsec_newlink` sets a new SecY operational before it has any SA, and an UPD_TXSA of the encoding SA sets operational to the SA's ACTIVE, so `set … tx sa 0 … off` reads back OPER 0 (`macsec_txsa_set`). `ip` does not print it; the test pins it.
- **`ip macsec`'s own refusals exit 1 or 255, and goip should match.** An SA with no key or no packet number, or a `set` with a key, fails `check_sa_args` and an unknown dev the dev lookup, each returning -1 (exit 1). SA number 4 is `invarg` (255). Each is after the family lookup, with nothing more sent to macsec.
- **`ip macsec` sends a salt and an SSCI only with `xpn`.** With `pn` the two words are parsed and dropped, so the 4-byte packet number on an XPN SecY reaches the kernel alone, which refuses it with EINVAL for its length (`macsec_xpn_txsa_pn32`).
- **No accepted offload mode change is captured.** `offload mac` and `offload phy` need a device with MACsec offload ops, and `d0` (a dummy) has none, so the kernel refuses `mac` (EOPNOTSUPP); `offload off` on a SecY already off is ACKed with nothing to change. Both send UPD_OFFLOAD, so the command has bytes; a device that takes the mode is a later task's, if goip needs it. macsec has no multicast group, so there are no notifications to capture.
- **`mptcp_monitor_conn`'s SF_CLOSED is a race.** V079's run closed the connection before the server's path manager logged its refused subflow back to the client, so it had 16 messages where the committed fixture has 17. The fixture, which has the event, stays; the C2-T04 decision that the server "logs an SF_CLOSED with ECONNREFUSED" holds for that fixture, not for every run, and the row's floor does not depend on it.
- **C2 closed without expanding C3.** C3's tasks were already one row each.
- **`ip xfrm state`'s own refusals send nothing and exit 1.** `ip/xfrm_state.c` checks its arguments (an ESP SA with no algorithm, `min` over `max`) before `rtnl_open_byproto`, unlike the genl tools, which have looked up their family first; the kernel's refusals exit 2. goip should match both.
- **allocspi asks for one SPI.** With a range the kernel picks the SPI at random, so the larval state would differ on every run; `min 0x200 max 0x200` keeps the fixture repeatable, and `allocspi_taken` covers a range with nothing free.
- **The time an SA was added is not pinned.** `ip -s` prints it and `curlft` carries it, so `TestXfrmStateCorpus` checks the lifetime limits and the byte and packet counts, not the add time.
- **XFRM returns keys.** Unlike macsec, a GETSA or dump answer carries the SA's keys, so the corpus's keys are fixed test values (every byte the same) and never anything real.
- **`ip xfrm policy`'s own refusals exit 1 or 255, and goip should match.** An add with no `dir`, and a get with neither selector nor index, return -1 from `ip/xfrm_policy.c` (exit 1). A bad `dir`, a selector and an index together, a hash threshold past the address length and the default action `drop` go through `invarg` or `duparg2` (exit 255). Each comes before `rtnl_open_byproto`, so nothing is sent; the kernel's refusals exit 2.
- **`xtcp2-nl-xfrmmon` waits on `/proc/net/netlink`, not a canary.** `ip xfrm monitor` prints nothing until an event arrives, and every XFRM group is below 32, so the helper waits for an XFRM socket (protocol 6) with a nonzero group mask before it runs the trigger. A canary would add an event of its own to every pcap.
- **The policy expire row uses a 3 s hard lifetime.** The policy timer is a timer-wheel timer, first armed a second after the add (`xfrm_policy.c`), and lifetimes are whole seconds, so with `time-hard 2` the first check can find the hard limit already passed and send only the hard POLEXPIRE. V088's first run did that twice. The SA timer is an hrtimer, and its 2 s stays.
- **The aevent row waits 1.2 s before its traffic.** The SA's replay timer is armed when the SA goes in, for `replay_maxage` (1 s), and finding nothing changed it only defers. A datagram after that sends NEWAE 0x20, "timer expired", and the third sends 0x10, a replay update. Without the wait the 0x20 depended on timing (one run of two), so the row waits and expects both.
- **MAPPING is captured, with a hand-built ESP-in-UDP datagram.** The plan expected to exclude it. `xtcp2-nl-espinudp` sends ESP from a port that is not the SA's encap port, to an ESPINUDP socket, for an SA with `cipher_null` (`crypto_null` is added to the late modules) and no auth, so no keys are needed and the kernel decrypts it and sends MAPPING. The ESP must be at least 20 bytes: `__xfrm4_udp_encap_rcv` compares `skb->len` against an IPv4 header length while `skb->data` is still the UDP header, and drops anything shorter before any lookup. A 12-byte ESP was dropped there (a guest `kfree_skb` trace, V089).
- **REPORT is not captured, and C4 lists it as an exclusion.** The kernel sends it only through `km_report`, which only `net/ipv6/mip6.c` calls, on Mobile IPv6 route-optimization paths that need a MIPv6 destination-option or routing-header state and a reject; no `ip` command reaches it.
- **A policy's index is the kernel's.** `ip` sends index 0, and `xfrm_gen_index` gives the first `out` policy in a fresh namespace 1, the first `in` 8, and a re-add after a delete 9. The rows run in fresh namespaces, so the indexes repeat, and the test pins them.
- **Sub-policies are not captured.** The guest kernel has no `CONFIG_XFRM_SUB_POLICY`, so `ptype sub` is refused, and `XFRMA_POLICY_TYPE` appears only as `main`.
- **`ip xfrm policy list dir in` is filtered by `ip`.** It sends the same GETPOLICY dump as a plain `list` and drops the other directions itself, so the pcap holds every policy and the output only `in`. goip should filter the same way.
- **The ACQUIRE's source port is masked.** The flow's selector carries the sending socket's ephemeral port, so `TestXfrmPolicyCorpus` and the comparison replace it, and pin the rest of the selector.
- **C3 closed without expanding C4.** C4 has one task, already its own row.
- **The rtnetlink universe is 44 types, not 45.** Written out as a literal list, iproute2 v7.1.0's `ip/` and `lib/` name 47 `RTM_*` types (not counting the `RTM_RTA` and `RTM_NHA` macros), three of them bridge-only, which is the 44 the assessment's table already listed. The plan's 45 and 48 were one too many.
- **Exclusions are listed, not left out.** The bridge-only dumps, DELNEXTHOPBUCKET, devlink's `CTRL_CMD_GETPOLICY` and XFRM's REPORT are in the universe and on the exclusion list with their reasons, so a capture of any of them makes the exclusion stale and fails the test.
- **A genl command counts only in a request.** Replies and events reuse command numbers (mptcp_pm's CREATED event is 1, as ADD_ADDR is), so the test counts a genl command only in a message with `NLM_F_REQUEST`, except the controller's NEWFAMILY, which is a reply. rtnetlink and XFRM types are counted in either direction, since `ip` names several only to print them.
- **The pcap walker stays.** The universe test counts only the universe; ERROR and DONE, the dedicated multicast and anycast dumps and the link-state bundles' families still need the walker, which How to re-measure keeps.
- **C4 closed by expanding P0.** Its range row became P0-T01 to P0-T04, one row per task in the plan's table.
- **P0 benchmarks read one table.** A parser can decode another type's body without an error, so a benchmark wired to the wrong type measures the wrong thing silently (V103). Each parser's type is in `parseBenchCases`, which every P0 benchmark and test reads, and `TestParseBenchCases` pins it.
- **A genl benchmark picks by family name.** Family ids are assigned at run time and differ between pcaps (ethtool is 22 in one and 23 in another), so a genl row names its family, and the corpus resolves each message's family from the NEWFAMILY replies in its own pcap.
- **The event benchmark runs over DELLINK.** `ParseRtnetlinkEvent` dispatches on eight types. The four NEW types are benchmarked through their own parsers, so the event benchmark takes a DEL type; DELLINK's picks (632 to 676 bytes) are full link bodies.
- **An equivalent mutant is recorded, not tested for.** `ParseEthtool` with `NLM_F_REQUEST` set decodes the three ethtool picks to the same kinds, so the benchmark measures the same work either way (V111).
- **P0 closed by expanding P1.** Its range row became P1-T01 to P1-T04, one row per task in the plan's table.
- **A benchmark may run over `ip`'s requests.** The kernel sends no LINKPROP message, only an ack and a NEWLINK, so `ParseLinkProp`'s picks are `ip`'s requests, chosen by a request kind; every other benchmark still runs over what the kernel sent.
- **`ParsePrefix` passes the flag byte through raw.** The kernel's `inet6_fill_prefix` copies the router advertisement's prefix option flags (on-link 0x80, autonomous 0x40), while `ip`'s `print_prefix` tests `IF_PREFIX_ONLINK` (0x01) and `IF_PREFIX_AUTOCONF` (0x02), so `ip` 7.1.0 prints neither for a real advertisement. The parser reports the wire byte; matching `ip`'s output, which never prints the flags, is goip's concern.
- **`ParsePrefix` has no allocation row.** It copies the address, 1 allocation on each pick; `TestParseBenchAllocs` holds only parsers allocation-free on some pick.
- **The done checkboxes are ticked late.** P0-T03 to P1-T03 were committed `done` with their box unticked; P1-T04's commit ticks them, with no other change to those rows.
- **P1 closed by expanding P2.** Its range row became P2-T01 to P2-T08, one row per genl family, in the C2 order.
- **A genl parser follows `ParseEthtool`, not the P1 rtnetlink idiom.** It builds on `ParseGenericNetlink`, so framing is checked as for every genl family, a repeated attribute is an error, and each scalar must be its exact width (`scalarAttribute`); optional fields are pointers. The cost is allocation: `ParseTCPMetrics` makes 20 per entry, where the P1 parsers make 0 to 2.
- **A P2 benchmark is per parser, not per command.** The plan's "a benchmark for each command" would add benchmarks over `ip`'s requests, a few attributes it builds itself. Each P2 parser has one benchmark over what the kernel sends, as every benchmark but LINKPROP's does, and its table test covers each command.
- **`ParseTCPMetrics` reports the metric values raw.** The kernel stores RTT scaled by 8 and RTTVAR by 4, and `ip` converts and shifts them for printing; the parser leaves that to its caller, as it leaves the prefix flags.
- **`ParseSeg6` reports SECRET and SECRETLEN as sent.** The kernel's answers carry a secret exactly SECRETLEN long, but `ip`'s delete sends SECRETLEN 0 and no SECRET, and its refused 65-byte set sends both at 65; `ip`'s `print_dumphmac` copies SECRETLEN bytes without checking SECRET. The parser does not reconcile them, so a caller sees what was on the wire.
- **The genl attribute decoder is shared.** P2-T01's `tcpMetricsDecoder` became `genlDecoder` in `xtcpnl_genetlink.go`, with `u8`, `s32` and `bytes`, so each later family's parser is a list of its fields; `ParseTCPMetrics` is unchanged in behavior and allocation.
- **IOAM6's TRACE event has its own parser.** IOAM6_EVENT_TRACE and IOAM6_CMD_ADD_NAMESPACE are both 1, and the event's attributes 2 to 4 are a u8, a u32 and binary where the commands' are a u32, a u64 and a u32, so one parser cannot read both. A kernel message of command 1 is the event, since the kernel never sends ADD_NAMESPACE; `ParseIOAM6Event` reads it, and the benchmarks pick by command (`genlCmds`).
- **mptcp_pm's events are told from its answers by the header.** The event numbers overlap the commands' (CLOSED 3 = GET_ADDR, ANNOUNCED 6 = GET_LIMITS), so a command filter, as IOAM6's, cannot split them. A multicast event has `nlmsg_pid` 0 and every kernel answer the requester's port id, dumps included, whose `nlmsg_seq` is 0 like an event's; the caller dispatches on `nlmsg_pid`, and the benchmarks pick by it (`genlAnswers`, `genlEvents`).
- **`genlDecoder` reads strings, and l2tp's counters by number.** l2tp's ifname is the first string a genl parser needs, so `genlDecoder` gains `str`, which requires the NUL, as the kernel's `nla_put_string` always sends it. The stats nest is family-specific and decoded by its own `l2tpStats`, which keeps the counters in their uapi numbers, so the pad at 9 is skipped rather than shifting RX_COOKIE_DISCARDS and RX_INVALID.
- **`ParseMacsec` reads list entries in order, whatever their numbers.** The kernel numbers a TXSA_LIST's, RXSC_LIST's or SA_LIST's entries 1, 2, … as it emits them, not by SA number; the parser keeps them as a slice in wire order and the SA number is the entry's own AN. An empty list, which the kernel sends for a SecY with no SA or channel, stays distinct from an absent one.
- **macsec's SCI and SSCI are in `ip`'s form.** Both are network order on the wire (`ip` prints them through `ntohll` and `ntohl`), so the parser reads them big-endian; the cipher suite and the PN are host order. A PN of 4 or 8 bytes is accepted, as the kernel's NLA_UINT policy does; whether 8 was right is the SecY's cipher suite's business, not the parser's.
- **P2 closed by expanding P3.** Its range row became P3-T01 to P3-T03, and the P3 table now assigns every XFRM type `ip` 7.1.0 names but REPORT, and NEWSADINFO, to one task, by its `xfrm_msg_min` body. NEWAE, FLUSHSA, FLUSHPOLICY, SETDEFAULT, GETDEFAULT, POLEXPIRE and MAPPING were in no task before.
- **An XFRM parser follows the rtnetlink idiom, not genl's.** The kernel reads `NETLINK_XFRM` with `nlmsg_parse_deprecated`, so `ParseXfrmState` walks its attributes with `WalkRTAttrs`: a repeated attribute keeps the last and an unknown one is skipped, as there. It is stricter on what it decodes: a struct attribute must be at least `xfrma_policy`'s size, a scalar exactly its width, and an algorithm must hold its key, so a short attribute is an `ErrXfrmAttr` rather than a slice past its end.
- **One SA parser takes the header.** Which struct leads an SA message, and whether GETSA is a dump with no struct, depend on `nlmsg_type` and `nlmsg_flags`, so `ParseXfrmState` takes the `NlMsgHdr`, as `ParseNlMsgErr` takes its flags. GETAE, which no task named, has NEWAE's body and is decoded with it; `ip` sends none, so a hand-built row covers it.
- **XFRM's shared pieces are exported in P3-T01.** The message types, the attributes, the struct sizes and the `XfrmAttrs` decoder are in `xtcpnl_xfrm.go`, so P3-T02's policies and P3-T03's events reuse them; the raw tests keep their own renderers, and constants for the SAD and SPD attribute spaces until P3-T03 exports them.
- **One policy parser takes the header too.** As with SAs, `nlmsg_type` picks the struct and `nlmsg_flags` makes a GETPOLICY a dump, so `ParseXfrmPolicy` takes the `NlMsgHdr`. FLUSHPOLICY has no struct (`xfrm_msg_min` gives 0), and a dump's attributes are read from the body, as for GETSA, although `xfrm_dump_policy` reads none.
- **An XFRMA_TMPL holds as many templates as fit.** `copy_from_user_tmpl` counts `nla_len / sizeof(struct xfrm_user_tmpl)`, so the parser does, leaving the bytes past the last template out. `XFRM_MAX_DEPTH` and the families `validate_tmpl` checks are the kernel's policy on a write, not part of the decode, so the parser does not enforce them.
- **SADINFO and SPDINFO have their own parser, and the kernel's events another.** Their bodies share nothing with an SA or a policy: the info types are a u32 of flags and attributes in the `XFRMA_SAD_*` and `XFRMA_SPD_*` spaces, which reuse `XFRMA_*`'s numbers, so `ParseXfrmInfo` decodes them; ACQUIRE, EXPIRE and MAPPING are each their own struct before `XFRMA_*` attributes, so `ParseXfrmEvent` decodes them through `XfrmAttrs`. ALLOCSPI's `xfrm_userspi_info` leads with an `xfrm_usersa_info`, so it stays in `ParseXfrmState`.
- **The acquire capture row masks two per-run values.** The ACQUIRE comes from a real flow, so its source port and the kernel's acquire sequence number change on every capture run; the row's expected text replaces them, and the hand-built rows check each field's offset.
- **The event benchmark runs over the one ACQUIRE.** The corpus holds one, so the smallest, typical and largest picks are the same 348 bytes; EXPIRE and MAPPING are covered by the tests.
- **P3 closed by expanding G0.** Its range row became G0-T01 to G0-T03, as the gaps doc's G0 table names them.
- **A row gates on runs whose control disagrees.** `-4 neigh show` read `nl=2` then `nl=6` on G0-T01's two runs, every value a link counter `D_control` subtracts, with no finding on either; it gates on G0-T01's bar, two runs passing with no finding and the same `txns`, as `-s link show` gated on runs that disagreed.
- **"Permanent" means no run and no allowlist entry can gate a row, not that no change to goip can.** Six of the eight permanent rows gated in G0-T02 once goip sent `ip`'s requests. The two netconf-dev rows stay in the set until G0-T03 decides them.
- **G0-T03 gates both held-out `-s` forms.** `-s neigh show` was held out to wait for a run where its control count moved. `-4 neigh show` sends the same link and neighbor dumps and showed that move, read `nl=2` then `nl=6`, and passed both runs. Waiting for `-s neigh show` itself to move is not a bar any other row was held to: `-d neigh show` gated at a steady `nl=2`.
- **A non-dump request with `NLM_F_ACK` closes on its ACK.** `netlink_rcv_skb` sends the ACK after the reply, so the reply alone is not the whole answer. The segmenter changes rather than allowlisting the orphans, which no allowlist entry can suppress anyway.
- **G0 closed by expanding G1.** G1's range row became G1-T01 to G1-T04, one task per verb family, as the gaps doc's G1 table names them.
- **`route get` refuses the arms it does not implement by name.** `tos`, `dsfield`, `connected`, `vrf`, `as`, `sport`, `dport`, `ipproto` and `flowlabel` return not implemented (exit 1) instead of being read as a destination, which is where `ip`'s chain sends an unknown word. None has a capture; each is a later task's.
- **The parity driver gains a sysctl Setup step, not a per-row hook.** The from/iif get is an input lookup, which needs forwarding on; `setup_step` runs `sysctl KEY VALUE` through `xtcp2-nlcap sysctl` as the corpus's `pre_step` does, so a row's preconditions stay one list.
- **The unnamed cache bits are text only.** `print_cache_flags` prints them in JSON with `print_hex` under a `"flags"` key inside the `"cache"` array, which is not valid JSON. The view keeps the names and drops the bits; the kernel sets neither bit (`include/uapi/linux/in_route.h`).
- **`neigh get` keeps `ip`'s two duplicate rules.** A second `dev` is no error and the last one wins; a second address is duparg, on `address` after `proxy` and on `to` otherwise. Both exit 1 in goip, as every duparg in goip does, where `ip`'s `exit(-1)` is 255.
- **goip asks for extended acks, as `ip` does.** The first G1-T02 run passed 190 of 191 and warned on `neigh_get_missing`: `ip`'s `ENOENT` reply set `NLM_F_ACK_TLVS` (0x0200) and carried "Neighbour entry not found", and goip's carried neither, because only `rtnl_open` sets `NETLINK_EXT_ACK` (`lib/libnetlink.c:222`). It is the first gated row whose refusal has an extack message. `OpenNetlink` now sets the option, ignoring `ENOPROTOOPT` as it does for strict checking, and both counted runs are after the change. goip still prints its own error wording on stderr, which the matrix does not compare.
- **`nexthop bucket show` refuses `dev`, `master` and `vrf` by name.** Each resolves a device before the dump, and no capture grounds that traffic; `id` and `nhid`, which go straight into the filter, are implemented. Each refused selector is a later task's.
- **The comparator models the bucket messages.** A first G1-T03 run passed 195 of 195 with no control noise on the bucket rows, which was too clean: `FamilyHdrLen` returned -1 for `RTM_*NEXTHOPBUCKET`, so `DecodeAttrs` left each bucket message's body as its remainder, a request was compared by body length alone and a reply's attributes not at all. A goip asking for group 21 would have passed. `FamilyHdrLen` now answers `struct nhmsg`'s 8 for the three bucket types, as the kernel fills them (`nh_fill_res_bucket`, `net/ipv4/nexthop.c`), and both counted runs are after the change. `nlparity_names.go` names no nexthop type or attribute, so the loci stay numeric (`type(118):attr(1)`); naming them is left to the task that next touches the names.
- **The bucket rows gate with `idle_time` moving.** A bucket's idle time counts up between `ip_a`, goip and `ip_b`, in its reply attribute and its printed `idle_time`. The comparator takes `NHA_RES_BUCKET` as one nested value, so each moved bucket is one `L3` value divergence, and the control, which sees the same movement between `ip_a` and `ip_b`, cancels it: 1024 on the dump and 1 on the get, on both runs, with no allowlist entry. The cancel is per locus, so a moved `nhid` in the same nest would be canceled too; the replies are the kernel's, and what binds goip is the request, compared byte for byte, the 1024-line count on stdout and `TestNexthopGetMatchesIp`'s offline equality with `ip.out`. `idle_time` is not one of the compared stdout keywords.
- **`link xstats` grounds the bridge kinds alone.** `bridge` and `bridge_slave` print `bridge_print_xstats`' stanzas, which the C1-T03 reads ground; the bond kinds print another layout with no capture, and goip cannot tell a kind iproute2 has without xstats from one it lacks, so every other kind is refused, not answered with either of `ip`'s two errors.
- **`dev` is refused on both verbs.** `link xstats … dev` and `link afstats dev` each resolve a name before the dump, and no capture grounds that lookup, as for `nexthop bucket show`.
- **The stp body is decoded, and `stats show` still refuses it.** `ParseNewStats` decodes `bridge_stp_xstats` for `link xstats type bridge_slave`; `stats show`'s stp leaf has no capture, so `statsShowXstats` refuses a record carrying one, as before.
- **`link afstats` refuses a record it would print for.** iproute2 prints an AF_SPEC member's counters for AF_MPLS and "unknown af" for any other; no capture holds a member, so a record with one of the family asked for is refused. A malformed member stops the walk without failing the record, as `RTA_OK` does.
- **G2 is three tasks.** `route showdump` sends nothing and reads the blob `route save` writes, so it ships with save (G2-T01); restore (G2-T02) and flush (G2-T03, closing G2) follow, each on its C1-T02 captures.
- **`link xstats type bridge` gates with the bridge's GC timer moving.** The row's name lookup returns br0's `RTM_NEWLINK`, whose `IFLA_BR_GC_TIMER` counts up between `ip_a`, goip and `ip_b`. The comparator takes `IFLA_LINKINFO` as one nested value, so it is one `L3` value divergence, canceled by the control, as with the bucket idle times; the request is compared byte for byte and the printed stanza does not carry the timer.
- **`route showdump` sends a get.** The gaps table and G2's expansion said showdump sends nothing; `print_route` names each device with `ll_index_to_name`, one `RTM_GETLINK` get per index not yet seen (V206). The showdump rows are therefore matrix rows, with that get compared, unlike `addr showdump`, which names no device.
- **A save row's stdout is compared as a dump file.** Its stdout is the kernel's replies, not text, so the comparator recognizes the magic and compares the records in order with seq and pid left out, under a new locus, `stdout:dumprecords`, which is presence class and cannot be allowlisted. Without it, the text facets would have counted whatever 0x0a bytes the sequence numbers held as lines.
- **The showdump rows read the committed file, not the save row's.** The runner carries `route_save`'s `ip.save` in, as it carries the address restore rows' files, so each showdump row's input is the capture's and not whatever an earlier row left behind. The rows' precondition is `route_save`'s, so d0 is ifindex 3 in the parity namespace, as in the file.
- **`xtcp2-nlcap` runs every row in the capture directory.** A read row's command ran in the driver's directory; it now runs where a write row's does, so a relative `< <slug>.save` resolves. No existing read row names a relative path, so no capture changes.
- **What showdump refuses.** `-j`, which `ip` answers with text since no JSON object is open; an `RTM_DELROUTE` record, which `ip` prints with `Deleted ` and a save never writes; and a record goip cannot decode. Each is exit 1, where `ip` exits 0.
- **`route restore` refuses what a dump could not have written.** `ip` sends any record the file holds, parsed as a route whatever its type. goip writes only `RTM_NEWROUTE` records it can classify, and refuses the file otherwise before sending anything, exit 1 where `ip` would have sent the record and taken the kernel's answer.
- **The restore row reads `route_save`'s file.** C1-T02's `route_restore` row restored a copy of `route_save`'s `ip.save`, byte-identical, so the matrix row reads the `route_save.save` the runner already carries for the showdump rows, and the runner gains nothing.
- **A dump that ends in an errno is an error, for goip alone.** The kernel answers a dump filtered to a table it does not have with ENOENT in the `NLMSG_DONE`; `rtnl_dump_done` reports it, and xtcpnl's walker read it as the end of an empty dump, so goip printed nothing and exited 0 where `ip` exits 2. `DumpRtnetlinkMsgsDoneErr` is opt-in: goip's sources use it, and `xtcp`'s and `rtnlmon`'s dumps keep the old reading, since a change there is a change to the daemon, not to parity. `route show` and `route save` keep `save_route_errhndlr`'s main-table exception; goip cannot tell an ENOENT DONE from an ENOENT error message, which the kernel does not send for a table filter.
- **`route flush` checks `GOIP_WRITE_OUT` at the first copy.** Address flush checks it before the first dump. Checking it when a round first has something to delete lets a flush that deletes nothing send what `ip` sends and exit as `ip` does, so the empty and missing-table captures are read rows, with no hook and nothing to write, and the two that delete are write rows.
- **The flush rows' Names abbreviate `table`.** A Name is a row's identity, and three C1-T02 captures ran `ip route flush table 100`; `tab` and `t` are the same bytes to `matches()`, and each Name says which capture it is by its Setup.
- **G3 is six tasks, one per object.** neigh, rule, nexthop and addrlabel each take their writes and flush, rule its save and restore too; ntable change and stats set, one write each, share a task; link property closes the phase.
- **A read row that `ip` could make write never runs on the shared topology.** G2-T03's first run put `route flush t 100` there, on the belief that the shared topology had no table 100; it has three routes in table 100, and `ip_a` deleted them, five transactions to `ip_b`'s one, and the row failed. The run is not counted; the row now has a one-step Setup, `link set lo up`, which gives it a namespace of its own, and the empty-table row already had one. A flush with nothing to delete is a read row only in a namespace whose table the row controls.
- **`neigh flush` keeps `NUD_NONE` entries.** Its `filter.state` is the int `~(NUD_PERMANENT|NUD_NOARP)`, so it carries the 0x100 bit `print_neigh` reads as keeping a state-zero entry; goip's first cut masked the state byte and skipped them. Checked on the host (V224) before the fix.
- **The neigh rows pin the veths' MACs and set `addrgenmode none`.** The C1 captures ran on a plain veth pair; in the matrix each side's namespace would give d0 its own MAC and link-local, whose solicited-node entries would sit in the neighbor dumps. `TestNeighWriteRows` holds each Setup to its capture's precondition less these changes.
- **The neigh flush rows' second entry is IPv6.** Two IPv4 entries dump in an order the kernel's per-namespace hash salt picks, so `ip_a`, `ip_b` and goip need not agree, and key order is unsuppressible. An `AF_UNSPEC` dump walks the ARP table before the ND table, so one entry per family is one order everywhere. The corpus capture keeps its two IPv4 entries; the unit tests replay it.
- **goip's neigh argument errors exit 1, `ip`'s 255.** `duparg`, `invarg` and the required-arguments refusal exit 255 in `ip`; goip keeps its convention that an argument error is exit 1, as for the address and route verbs. The matrix compares no refusal.
- **`rule flush` refuses selectors.** `ip` filters the dump's replies by them in `filter_nlmsg`, which changes which copies go out but no request byte goip could be compared on without a capture of it; as `rule show` refuses them, goip refuses them until one is captured.
- **goip's rule argument errors exit 1.** `ip` exits 255 on `invarg`, 22 on the l3mdev table refusal and 1 on "requires arguments."; goip keeps its convention, as for the neigh, address and route verbs. The matrix compares no refusal.
- **`ipproto` and `dsfield` names come from tables in goip.** `ip` reads `/etc/protocols` and `rt_dsfield`; goip reads no config file, so it carries `rt_dsfield` as iproute2-7.1.0 ships it and the common IP protocol names, and refuses a name outside them as not implemented. `realms`, which reads `rt_realms`, and `nat`, which the kernel no longer builds, are not implemented.
- **`-j rule save` and `-j rule flush` are refused.** `new_json_obj` puts `[]` on stdout around each, and no capture shows it.
- **The nexthop flush row flushes the two singles alone.** `nexthop_flush`'s capture holds resilient group 20, deleted between the groups dump and the full dump, so its writes are two runs, and a write row's writes must be one (`nlparity.SplitWrites`). The matrix row's Setup is `nexthop get`'s, two single nexthops: two dumps, then two deletes. `TestNexthopFlushMatchesIp` still checks the whole capture, group 20 between the dumps, and the comparator test reads the row from that capture less the group's reply, delete and ack.
- **`nexthop flush` refuses selectors, `id` and `-s`.** Each changes the dumps (a filter attribute, or `NHA_OP_FLAGS` for `-s`) or, for `id`, makes the flush one delete, and no capture shows any of them; goip refuses them before any request until one is captured.
- **`encap`, `dst_port` and `hw_stats` are not implemented on nexthop writes.** No capture shows them, so goip refuses them before any request.
- **goip's nexthop argument errors exit 1.** `ip` exits 255 on `invarg` and 1 on a gateway it cannot read in the request's family; goip keeps its convention, as for the neigh, rule, address and route verbs. The matrix compares no refusal.
- **`addrlabel` writes skip an unknown word, as `ip` does.** `ipaddrlabel_modify`'s loop has no `else`, so `ip addrlabel add bogus prefix … label …` sends the request; goip models that rather than refusing, the one write parser here that does.
- **goip's error strings drop `ip`'s trailing period.** `ip` ends "Not enough information" and `get_prefix`'s refusals with one; staticcheck's ST1005 and revive's error-strings forbid it, so goip's messages are `ip`'s less that period, here and in the route, rule and nexthop verbs before it. The matrix compares no refusal.
- **The addrlabel flush copy keeps the reply's seq.** `ip` sends each copy from a socket it opens per reply, at that socket's seq; the comparator zeroes seq and port id, so goip keeps the reply's, as `rule flush` does.
- **`ntable change` and `stats set` refuse as `ip` does but exit 1.** `ip` exits 255 on its `invarg`, `duparg` and missing-argument refusals, 22 (`-EINVAL`) on `stats set`'s bad value, second suite and unknown word, and 1 on an unknown device; goip exits 1 on each, its convention. The matrix compares no refusal.
- **`stats set … help` is not implemented.** `ip` prints its usage and exits 0 wherever `help` falls, sending nothing; goip refuses it, exit 1, as it does `help` on every object.
- **`ntable change` with `dev` alone changes nothing.** `dev` fills `NDTPA_IFINDEX` but sets no `parms_change`, so `ip` refuses the request; goip models that rather than sending an `NDTA_PARMS` that names a device and nothing else.
- **`link property` exits 1 on its refusals and does not implement `help`.** `ip` exits 255 on its argument refusals and on `help`, and 1 on an unknown device; goip exits 1 on each, its convention. The matrix compares no refusal.
- **`link property dev` with a name only an altname lookup can find is not implemented.** The loop checks the device with `check_altifname`, so `dev a/b` passes it, and `ll_name_to_index` then asks the kernel for a link with that altname (`IFLA_ALT_IFNAME`), which answers `Cannot find device`, rc 1. goip's name resolver sends the by-name `IFLA_IFNAME` get alone and refuses the altname lookup as not implemented, also exit 1, until a capture shows that get.
- **G4 is six tasks, one per object.** mroute and mrule are reads; token and netns each take a read and a write; the rule notifications become events in `pkg/xtcpnl` before `monitor`, which renders every captured notification and closes the phase.
- **`mroute` exits 1 on its usage refusals and does not implement `help`.** `ip` exits 255 on `help`, an unknown verb, a keyword with no value and a bad table, and 1 on a bad prefix, an unknown `iif` and a failed dump; goip exits 1 on each, its convention.
- **`mroute show iif` resolves the name from `ll_init_map`'s cache alone.** `ll_name_to_index` would send a live by-name get on a cache miss; goip refuses the miss as `Cannot find device`, the position `neigh show` takes. `ip`'s rc for an unknown `iif` is 1 as well.
- **`mroute show table main` stderr differs.** The kernel refuses a missing MR table with ENOENT and the extack "ipv4: MR table does not exist."; `ip` prints the extack and `Dump terminated`, goip its own error, both exit 1. The matrix compares no refusal.
- **`mroute`'s `-s` row is gated though its `Age` ticks.** No stdout facet reads the age, and the counters and the line structure are the same on every side; the grounding compared the `-s` text and JSON with the age alone differing.
- **`mrule` reuses `rule show` whole, family aside.** `do_multirule` only swaps `preferred_family` before `do_iprule`, so goip's `runMrule` sets the family and calls `ruleShow`, keeping `rule show`'s render, its refusal of every selector, and its refusal of `FRA_IP_PROTO` and `FRA_DSCP`; no ipmr rule can carry either, since `ipmr_rule_fill` adds neither.
- **`mrule` implements the listing only.** `ip mrule add`, `del`, `flush`, `save` and `restore` run `do_iprule`'s writes with the multicast family; goip refuses each as not implemented, exit 1, as it does `help` and an unknown verb, where `ip` exits 255. No matrix row covers an mrule write.
- **`mrule`'s family refusal exits 255, as `ip`'s does.** `do_multirule` prints "Multicast rules are only supported for IPv4/IPv6, was: N" and calls `exit(-1)`; goip prints the same text after its `goip:` prefix and exits 255 (`ErrMruleFamily`). It is unit-tested, not a matrix row: neither side sends a request, and a row needs a floor of at least one datagram.
- **`token` exits 1 on its usage refusals and does not implement `help`.** `ip` exits 255 on `help`, an unknown verb, a `dev` it cannot resolve and a `dev` with no name; goip exits 1, as for `mroute`.
- **`token`'s `dev` resolves from `ll_init_map`'s cache alone.** `ll_name_to_index` sends a by-name `RTM_GETLINK` on a miss, then tries `if_nametoindex`; goip refuses the miss without either, as `mroute show iif` does.
- **`token` prints IPv4-compatible tokens as glibc does.** `inet_ntop` ends an address whose first six words are zero and seventh is not in dotted quad, and the kernel keeps only a token's low 64 bits, so any token of the form `::a:b` reaches that branch; `render.inet6Ntop` reproduces it for tokens. The other renderers keep netip's form, which differs on such an address; none is on an interface in the corpus.
- **`token` refuses a token that is not 16 bytes.** `inet_ntop` reads 16 bytes whatever the attribute holds; goip ends the dump instead. The kernel always sends 16.
- **`token del` has no matrix row.** C1-T02 captured `set` on a veth and on a dummy and no `del`; `del` is the same request with a zero token, which `TestTokenSet` pins. The refused row is spelled `token add`, since two rows cannot share a command, and sends the same request as `set`.
- **`RTM_SETLINK` is an admitted write type.** `ip token` is the only `ip` command that sends it; `link set` sends `RTM_NEWLINK`, and the gate test's row saying so was replaced by `RTM_SETLINK`'s admission.
- **A read row's Setup may run a daemon.** The `smcroute` step holds smcrouted in the row's namespace, because the kernel drops an (S,G) entry when the socket that added it closes; `xtcp2-nlcap ns-add` and `ns-del` stop a held daemon, so it does not outlive its namespace.
- **`NETNSA_FD` is the allowlist's first accepted divergence.** `ip netns list-id` and `ip netns set` name a namespace to the kernel by a descriptor of its file, a slot in the sender's own table; goip, a Go process, holds other descriptors than `ip` does. Matching the number would mean padding the table, a fake, so each `netns` row carries an accepted-divergence entry for the attribute and every other byte is still compared. The four `list-id` entries did not fire in the VM, both sides opening each file as 6; they stay, since that rests on the Go runtime's descriptor count.
- **`netns` implements `list-id` and `set` only.** `list`, `add`, `delete`, `exec`, `identify`, `pids`, `monitor` and `attach` are mounts, `setns` and `/proc` reads; goip refuses each as not implemented, exit 1, as it does `help`. Usage refusals exit 1 where `ip` exits 255, and `-o` is refused, as for `token`.
- **`netns set`'s path branch is unreachable.** `set_netns_id_from_name` opens a name containing a slash as a path, but `do_netns`'s `invalid_name` refuses any such name first, short of `ip -all`, which goip does not implement; goip joins the name to `/run/netns` alone.
- **`netns` serves `/ip/netns` through `list-id`.** An object's show verb was `show` for every object; dispatch now carries a per-object show verb, so `Show` and the HTTP index run `netns list-id`.
- **The parity driver's `peer NAME ?ID?` setup step.** A row's namespace needs named peers with nsids for `list-id`, so the driver adds the namespace under `/run/netns` and, given an ID, sets its nsid with `ip netns set`, the step `rtnl-read-corpus.exp` used for the capture; `TestNetnsRows` pins the two.
- **Rule notifications are events, and the daemon is unchanged.** `RuleEvent` joins the four records; `pkg/rtnlmon` subscribes to no rule group, and each of its handlers ignores an event type it does not handle, so the classification adds nothing to the daemon's stream.
- **`RTM_DELRULE` as an event is covered by a constructed row.** C1-T04's `monitor_all` captured `ip rule add` and no delete; the body is `fib_rule_hdr` and `FRA_*`, which `ParseRule` reads from 21 captured dump replies, so a capture of the delete would add no decoding the corpus does not already exercise.
- **`goip monitor` has no matrix row.** The parity driver runs no `ip monitor`, by design: a multicast notification inside a read-only capture window is a hygiene failure (`goip-parity.exp`). Its evidence is the seven C1-T04 and `addr-monitor` captures replayed through `Run` and a live comparison against iproute2-7.1.0, both kept in the ledger.
- **A replayed monitor models `ip`'s dump window.** `ll_init_map` dumps on the subscribed socket and `rtnl_dump_filter` skips what is not the dump's, so `ip` loses the notifications that arrive during it; `prefix_event`'s first join is one. The replay starts after the link dump's `NLMSG_DONE`, which the capture records, rather than playing every notification.
- **`monitor` is left out of `Show` and the HTTP paths.** It never ends; `object.noShow` keeps it out of `ShowObjects`, so goiphttp registers no `/ip/monitor`.
- **The monitor's messages differ from `ip`'s in three ways, and its exits do not.** goip prefixes `goip: monitor: `, drops `ip`'s trailing period (ST1005), and prints Go's lowercase errno text where `perror` capitalizes it.
- **G5 is three tasks.** The comparator learns genl first, since nothing else can be checked without it; goip's transport second; the harness third, with one ungated `ip`-against-`ip` row per family, so G6's tasks each implement a family and gate its rows.
- **A genl message's kind is its family's name and command.** The id is the boot's, so `pkg/nlparity` names a family from the capture's own `CTRL_CMD_NEWFAMILY` replies and compares `GENL(<family>).cmd(<n>)`. A family no reply names compares by id, the old comparison, rather than being guessed; a capture naming one id twice is refused.
- **A genl reply keys on its ordinal until its family gives it a key.** Grouping a dump's entries under one key skips their comparison when the counts differ; numbering them makes one more entry a key-set finding. G6 adds each family's identity where the ordinal proves too strict.
- **An ACK's errno stays out of L3.** An `NLMSG_ERROR` body decodes to no attributes, on rtnetlink as on genl, and the exit status, compared beside the netlink, carries a refusal. G5-T01 leaves that as it is.
- **goip's GETFAMILY is version 0, `ip`'s.** `BuildGetFamilyRequest` sends version 2, which `pkg/linkmonitor` keeps; the controller does not read it, but the parity comparator compares the genlmsghdr, so goip sends `GENL_REQUEST`'s 0 through `BuildGetFamilyRequestVersion`.
- **A genl replay answers by request bytes.** The route replay answers by reply type and portid; a genl request is matched to the recorded exchange whose request is the same bytes, so a request `ip` did not send has no answer, and each exchange replays once.
- **A genl family goip lacks is compared `ip` against `ip` until G6.** A row with no goip capture compares nothing. Running `ip` in goip's slot puts the genl capture, the comparator's labels and the driver's late setup through a live run before any goip object exists. The row stays ungated, so a stand-in can never be counted as goip's parity.
- **The table's line has nine fields, `proto` before `setup`.** The driver refuses any other count, so a driver and a table that disagree fail at the first line, not partway through.
- **The modules load before the first genl row, not at boot.** Loading them at boot changes the route rows' ifindexes (`mkVm.nix`'s measured note, as at C0-T02), so the table puts genl rows last and the driver loads them on reaching the first. `TestGenlStandInRows` pins the order.
- **`token_set`'s veths take no link-local address.** Its link dump raced DAD and the router solicitation. `addrgenmode none` on both veths, as the neigh write rows have, removes the traffic, and the kernel still takes the token, since it checks only `IFF_NOARP`, `accept_ra` and `rtr_solicits`.
- **A genl write is admitted by family name and command.** A family's id is the boot's, so the gate cannot hold a number for it. `IsAdmittedGenlWrite` admits tcp_metrics' DEL alone; each G6 task admits its own family's writes.
- **tcp_metrics' measured values are not compared.** Each side of a row opens its own connections, so an entry's age, last SYN drop stamp and RTTs differ by timing alone, and the two `ip` sides can agree by chance where goip's does not. `normalizeTCPMetrics` zeroes them. The addresses, cwnd, ssthresh and the rest stay compared.
- **A tcp_metrics dump's order is not compared.** The kernel walks a hash keyed per namespace, so every write row's sides dump the same entries in different orders. Replies and a flush's DELs pair by family, address and source instead; a missing or extra entry still shows as a key on one side only.
- **The two corpus commands whose names another row has are spelled `list` and with `address`.** `tcpm_show_empty` and `tcpm_flush_empty` run `ip`'s other keyword for the same request bytes, since a row's name is its identity in the allowlist and in `Lookup`.
- **`goip tcp_metrics` exits 255 where `ip` exits `-1`.** Its usage refusals wrap `ErrTCPMetricsUsage`, as rule's and addrlabel's flush errors are mapped, so a script sees `ip`'s status. `help` is the exception: `ip` prints usage and exits 255, and goip, which prints no usage, refuses it as not implemented, exit 1.
- **goip's parse refuses what `ip`'s refuses.** `ip`'s loop steps past the word after each address and source value, so `address A src B` reads `B` as a second address and is a duplicate. goip keeps the quirk rather than accept a command `ip` refuses.
- **The HTTP path takes the family alone.** `/ip/tcpmetrics` serves `ip -j tcp_metrics show`, the dump; a get prints text in `ip`, not JSON, so the address and source selectors are not offered.
- **The genl socket has a receive timeout.** It had none, so a family that never answered would hang goip; it now takes the route socket's.
- **`sr hmac set` reads its secret as `getpass` does.** `ip` calls glibc's `getpass`, which prompts and reads on `/dev/tty` with echo off, or with no terminal prompts on stderr and reads stdin. goip does the same, through the same terminal settings, rather than read stdin always: run at a terminal, a secret read with echo on would be shown.
- **A matrix row can carry stdin.** The secret is not in the argv, so a row gets a `Stdin` line, printed as the table's tenth field, and the driver pipes it to each side under `setsid -w`, as the corpus did. Only letters and digits are allowed, since the driver passes it through the shell unquoted.
- **The table's line has ten fields, `stdin` after `setup`.** It is `-`, or `>` and the line, so a blank line, the delete, is `>` and not mistaken for none.
- **The three corpus commands whose names another row has are spelled with `ip`'s abbreviations.** `sr hmac s`, `sr hm show` and `sr hmac se` send the same bytes as `show` and `set`.
- **`sr` writes its JSON as `jsonw_puts` does.** `encoding/json` escapes `<`, `>` and `&` and replaces bytes that are not UTF-8; `ip` does neither, and `print_dumphmac` prints `<unknown>` and secrets that may hold any byte.
- **`sr` has no HTTP path.** `hmac show` prints the HMAC secrets, which the read-only HTTP service should not serve, and `tunsrc show` alone is not worth a path; `sr` is left out of the show objects.
- **`goip sr` exits as `ip` does but for `help`.** Usage refusals wrap `ErrSrUsage`, exit 255. `ip sr`, `ip sr help` and `ip sr h`, which `matches` reads as help before hmac, print usage and exit 255; goip prints no usage and refuses them as not implemented, exit 1, as for tcp_metrics.
- **The ioam monitor is not in the matrix.** The parity driver runs no monitor, as G4-T06 recorded, so `ioam monitor`'s three corpus rows are checked by replay, the TRACE row byte for byte, and live by its requests and group, not as matrix rows.
- **The genl monitor shares `rtnl_listen`'s loop.** `goip monitor`'s receive loop is now a function both monitors call, so ENOBUFS is reported and retried the same way on the genl socket.
- **Each `-j` ioam show holds one entry.** The kernel keeps IOAM namespaces and schemas in an rhashtable seeded per network namespace, so a dump of several can come back in another order in `ip`'s namespace than in goip's. The `-j` rows show one bound namespace and its schema; the readbacks of several are replayed.
- **The three ioam corpus commands whose names another row has are spelled with base-0 ids.** `ioam` matches keywords by `strcmp`, so `ip`'s abbreviations are not open to it; `0x1` and `01` send the same bytes as `1`.
- **`goip ioam` exits as `ip` does, `help` included.** Unlike tcp_metrics and sr, ioam's usage is `strcmp`'s plain `help` or no verb, so goip refuses both with the usage text, exit 255.
- **Duplicate genl accessors are one.** `srGenl` and `tcpmGenl` were the same function; `genlSource` replaces both, and the -j array helpers sr introduced are shared as `genlJSONAppend` and `genlJSONEnd`.
